LOCAL NEWS. (Contintiod front .Page 5.) Personal. A marriage will take place quietly on January sth,l between Arthur Henry, elder son of the late Hon. Conrad Dillon, and Hilda, widow of Harold Broadbent and daughter of the Right Hon. Sir John Brunner, Bart., M.P. , We regret to announce that the Chief Constable, Mr. Luke Talbot, is lying ill at his residence, Palmyra- square. He is suffering from a low fever, and whilst his condition is grave, we are pleased to learn that there is every prospect of an ultimate recovery. Mr. Fairfield Turner, the well know Warrington athelete, was seized with a sudden illness while watching the " A " team match at Warrington on Saturday. He is now reported to be much better. i Women's Suffrage. An interesting debate on this question took place at the meeting of Bewsey-road Wesley Guild on Monday. The affirmative was introduced by Dr. Ferguson who was supported by Miss L. Pitt and Miss Rowlands. The argument for the negative was led by Mr. Seed, and Miss Prince. Mr. Mitchell and Mr. Prince followed on the same lines. The case against the granting of the vote to women was carried by a narrow. majority. The Men's Meetings. " Does death end all ? " was the subject of the address at the Men's Meeting iu Toft's Assembly Room on Thursday evening. Mr. Matthews gave the answer of science to the question, and stated that with i the one exception of "No waste or loss in the ' Universe" science had practically nothing to say. He reviewed the answer which Christ offered to the question, and suggested that whatever comfort was needed by the human spirit was to be found there if anywhere. Many questions were asked at the close. Shorthand Writers' Association. The weekly meeting of the Warrington and District Shorthand Writers' Association was held at the Winmarleigh Cafe on Wednesday night last, a fair number being preset. After speed practice, the following papers were given :—" The arrangement of Newspaper Cuttings," 1:ly Mr. T. Collins," Home," by Miss Smith, " The Shorthand Clerk," by Mr. Joseph Marsh, and "Are Theatres Demoralising ?"by Miss E. Houghton. These short essays proved very interest- ing, and a discussion followed the reading of each. A hearty vote of thanks to the respective readers con- cluded the proceedings. ' Latchford Wesleyan Church. The united Bible classes held a tea and concert in the Parish room on Thursday evening. Songs were ably rendered by the following artistes : —Misses Mabel Henshall, Peacock, and Barnes, and Mr. J. Willett, while the humourons element was well sus- tamed by Mr. Alf. Grounds. A sketch entitled " A sprig of holly " was capitally reproduced by the following : Messrs, Percy Ford, Wearell, Fletcher, Horton, Frederick Blease, and the Misses Ethel and .N. Ford, H. Gaskell, N. Mail, and E. Gaskell. The singing of the National Anthem brought a pleasant evening to a close. The concert was in aid of the inew Sunday School. ! Popular Penny Concerts. The ccntinned popularity of these weekly concerts was emphasised on Saturday when there was again a crowded attendance in the Bold-street schools. An, admirable enteitainment was progided, the pro- gramme being sustained by a special 'party from Mm- chester. The Lieidertafel Glee Singers and Mr. Houghton Brazendale's Manchester Concert Party contributed several well rendered quartettes and part songs, including " Comrades in arms," " The lover's complaint," " Annie Laurie," " The soldier's fare- well" and " The three chafers." Songs were also given in an able manner by the Misses Palmer and Wilkinson, and Messrs. H. BrazendaTe, S. Shaw and P. Walker, whilst Miss Nellie Gibson was warmly applauded for two pianoforte solos. The humorist was Mr. Arthur Statham, who kept the audience highly amused. Xmas PRESENTS in large variety, see windows.— J. S. GARLAND, 51 to 55, Bridge St., Warrington.— Advt. Ellesmere-street Wesleyan Band of Hope. The first annual tea and concert in con- nection with the above Society was held on Monday, tea being provided for the children at five o'clock and for adults at six o'clock. Mr. J. T. Rowland presided _ over the entertainment and said be was glad that they were " whole-hoggers " in regard to the temperance question. It was only by becoming extremists in the matter that they could de anything to clear away the terrible blight of intemperance.— The musical part of the programme was provided by Mr. P. L. Richardson, and included songs, duets, and trios, the artistes being Miss L. Duckworth, G. Ballow, D. 011iver, A. Beswick, E. Millard, and P Taylor. Mr. Levi Richardson also contributed two solos in his usual excellent manner. The humour of the evening was provided by Mr. Bert Spruce, whose sketches caused much amusement.— During an interval Mr. J. Barlow presented a number of prizes to the junior members, who during the session 1905-6 had merited them either by good conduct or reciting. Brief speeches were delivered by Mr. J. Hamblett, Mr. F. Cox and Mr. M. H. Llewellyn, all bearing testimony to the progress of the Society and its work. ' Friendly Societies' Representative on the Education Committee. A meeting of the various Friendly Societies was held at the Town Hall on Monday evening for the purpose of recommending a member to sit on the Education Committee, a vacancy having arisen owing to Mr. Steel's retirement on his election as a Councillor. Thirty-five delegates were in attendance representing over 8,000 members. The following Societies were presented, viz. :—Rechabites, Druids, National Order of Oddfellows, Forresters, Grand United Order of Oddfellows, Shepherds (Ashton Unity), Independent Ord'er of Oddfellows (Manchester Unity), Hearts of Oak, Irish National Foresters, Ancient Order of Shepherds and Independent Order of Foresters. P.G.M. T. P. Bradshaw was appointed chairman and the election was by ballot. The following nominations were handed in :—Bro. Peter Peacock, P.C.R., of the Foresters and Bro. I', P. Bradshaw, G.M. of the Independent Order of Oddfellows., The voting 'resulted as follow. —Bro. Bradshaw, 19; Bro. Peacock, 10. re was then unanimously resolved that P.G.M. Bradshaw's name be recommended to the Town Council as the representntive of the Friendly Societies on the Education Committee. The success- ful candidate having briefly thanked the members for the honour conferred upon him, a vote of thanks was accorded to the Mayor for the use of the Town Hall and to Mr. T. H. Spinks, Assistant Town Clerk, for j his attendance at the meeting. '
